# Historical claim ledger — Dutch hymn project

Snapshot: 25 October 2026. This is an initial claim register, not a claim that the entire field has been researched exhaustively. The repository root evidence policy controls source classes and operational states.

Claim status is a project-local review label, not a new source class and not the global publicationState. Source IDs refer to data/source-registry-v1.json.

## CLM-L-1676-IMPRINT — Uyt-spanningen first publication

**Claim:** The historical title page identifies Uyt-spanningen as printed and sold by Willem Clerck in Utrecht in 1676 and frames the collection as edifying songs and other poems.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676, SRC-L-1676-COLOPHON

**Limits / next action:** The digital presentation is explicitly diplomatic and has omissions/corrections; use page scans for exact material/typographic claims.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

The two DUTCH-001 locator decisions below were reconciled on 2026-11-18 with the [earlier first-edition transcription check](DOSSIER_DUTCH-001_TRANSLATION_DECISIONS.md). They do not record a new inspection of the printed leaves.

## CLM-DUTCH-001-TITLE-TUNE — Schepselen niet en Jesus al title and tune

**Claim:** DBNL's diplomatic transcription of the 1676 edition gives the title Schepselen Niet en Iesus Al and the tune direction 'Singt als: Rosemont waar gy vliedt'; the 2005 critical edition (item 51) reads 'waer'.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-SCHEPSELEN-131-134, SRC-L-SCHEPSELEN-51, SRC-MUSIC-ROSEMONT

**Limits / next action:** The first-edition heading is located in the transcription of printed pp. 131–134 (DBNL item 0056); this records a direction, not the melody or the sound of a modern arrangement. Typography still requires an image.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-001-DATE — Date attributed to Schepselen niet en Jesus al

**Claim:** The closing date line in the DBNL transcription of the 1676 edition reads 'Slagt-m. 1661' (November 1661), consistent with the date in the 2005 critical edition.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-SCHEPSELEN-131-134, SRC-L-SCHEPSELEN-51

**Limits / next action:** The locator is the end of the poem on printed pp. 131–134 in DBNL item 0056. This is the date attached to the poem, not the 1676 publication date. No page image was inspected.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HERT-I-VS-II — Two poems titled Hert-sterckte in Jehova

**Claim:** Lodenstein's collection contains I. Hert-sterckte in Jehova (critical item 34, dated 28 December 1673 in the critical edition) and II. Hert-sterckte in Jehova (item 35), which is the source associated with the later Rutgers hymn no. 68.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-HERT-I-1673-34, SRC-L-HERT-II-1674-1971, SRC-RUTGERS-1806-68, SRC-RUTGERS-BIO

**Limits / next action:** Keep the two poems separate. The first has its own tune directions and must not be silently treated as the direct source text for the current Russian hymn.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1674 — Date of II. Hert-sterckte in Jehova

**Claim:** The later digital witness reads 4 January 1674, but the source/editorial note records an erroneous 1664 in the original print and a correction to 1674.

**Review state:** EVIDENCE_HOLD

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-HERT-II-1674-1971, SRC-L-1676-COLOPHON

**Limits / next action:** Do not present 4 January 1674 as a clean, unproblematic date from the original print until the page image is visually checked and the emendation documented. The date HOLD applies to this claim, not to all other content in the poem.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-002-RUTGERS — Source of Russian hymn Блаженно, блаженно — быть ничем

**Claim:** The Russian version follows the later seven-stanza Zelfverloochening in Evangelische Gezangen (1806), hymn LXVIII, associated with Jodocus van Lodenstein material revised by Abraham Rutgers; it is not a word-for-word translation of the whole 1674 poem.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-RUTGERS-1806-68, SRC-RUTGERS-BIO, SRC-L-HERT-II-1674-1971

**Limits / next action:** Credit Lodenstein as historical source author and Rutgers as later reviser; treat later translations/adaptations as separate textual witnesses. The separate date uncertainty is isolated in CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1674; it does not automatically suspend the source-chain claim.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-AFR-L276-CHAIN — Afrikaans transmission

**Claim:** A modern institutional song page lists an adaptation chain from Lodenstein/Rutgers through Gerdener (1931), Pienaar/AGB (1944), Van der Merwe (1978) and Breytenbach (2001).

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_BY_SECONDARY_SOURCE_NEEDS_COLLATION

**Source IDs:** SRC-AFR-L276, SRC-AFR-L271

**Limits / next action:** Use this as a discovery/corroboration path; verify each intermediary edition before publishing detailed claims about exact text changes. The currently associated melody is later and is not evidence of an original 1674 tune.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-REVIUS-1630 — Hy droech onse smerten: first publication and genre

**Claim:** Hy droech onse smerten is a fourteen-line Passion sonnet included in the 1630 collection; composition date and original melody are not established by the sources currently acquired.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_BY_SECONDARY_SOURCE_NEEDS_COLLATION

**Source IDs:** SRC-R-REVIUS-AUTHOR, SRC-R-OVER-YSSEL-CONTENTS, SRC-R-HY-DROEG, SRC-R-OVER-YSSEL-1630-WORK

**Limits / next action:** The exact online poem text is from the 1930–1935 scholarly edition, not a direct scan of the 1630 page. Do not conflate publication year with composition date.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, LOCATOR_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-UTRECHT-1615-FACSIMILE — The Utrecht Hymni songbook and access layer

**Claim:** The original Hymni songbook is dated 1615, but the accessible DBNL page is a copyrighted 1967 facsimile edited by Jan van Biezen and Marie Veldhuyzen.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-UTRECHT-HYMNI-1615-1967, SRC-UTRECHT-HYMNI-1615-WORK

**Limits / next action:** The prefatory appeal to Ephesians 5:19 can be discussed with this edition caveat. Do not describe the current digital page as an independent scan of the 1615 original.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## Third-pass additions — 2026-10-25

Added after reading the 1676 printing directly (both Hert-sterckte poems and *Den nedrigen christen*), the 1727 ninth edition, the 2005 introduction and the 1971 anthology. `projectLevel: true` marks a claim that belongs to the corpus or the project method rather than to a single work.

## CLM-HERT-II-SEQUEL — Relation between I. and II. Hert-sterckte in Jehova

**Claim:** II. Hert-sterckte is a companion poem written one week after I: it carries no tune of its own but a printed pointer to I on p. 78, and its opening takes up the closing image of I.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HERT-I-78-81, SRC-L-1676-HERT-II-82-84, SRC-L-UYT-1727, SRC-L-CRIT-2005

- The tune pointer ('als boven pag.78') is confirmed in both the 1676 and the 1727 prints and is described the same way in the 2005 apparatus.
- The verbal continuation is established by the 2005 apparatus cross-referencing I, r. 89 ('In dien Ocean') for the opening word of II; that cross-reference is B1, but the two stanzas themselves corroborate it.
- Do not describe the two poems as recensions of one text, or one as a draft of the other.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HERT-I-DATE-1673 — Date of I. Hert-sterckte in Jehova

**Claim:** The 1676 print dates I. Hert-sterckte in Jehova at Fort Nieuw Rees, 28 December 1673.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HERT-I-78-81, SRC-L-HERT-I-1673-34

- The printed date line reads '28. in wintermaand 1673'; 'wintermaand' is December.
- No emendation of this date is listed in the DBNL colophon.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1727-WITNESS — Independent printed witness for the 4 January 1674 date

**Claim:** The 1727 ninth edition independently typesets II. Hert-sterckte with the closing line 'Op 't Fort Nieuw Rees 4 in Loumaand 1674'.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-UYT-1727

- A different setting of type, 51 years later; it is a printed A1 witness that reads 1674.
- It may descend from a corrected copy of the first edition, so it corroborates rather than closes the question of what the 1676 leaf reads.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HERT-EMENDATION-SCOPE — Scope of the recurring 1664->1674 emendations

**Claim:** The DBNL colophon's second 1664->1674 correction, at printed p. 87, belongs to item 36 ('t Verheven Herte, dated 11 January 1674) and not to II. Hert-sterckte; a third related correction is 'p. 7: 1565 -> 1665'.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-COLOPHON, SRC-L-1676-VERHEVEN-85-87, SRC-L-1676-HERT-II-82-84

- Prevents the date hold from being applied to the wrong poem, and prevents the p. 87 correction from being used as a second witness for II.
- The recurrence of the same digit error at several dates is consistent with a compositor-level fault rather than an isolated slip, but this is an inference about printing practice, not a proof of any single reading.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-REES-MEDITATIEN-1674 — The four-poem Rees group and the 1674 pamphlet

**Claim:** Items 34-37 of the 1676 collection are the group of consolation songs written during Lodenstein's December 1673 hostage exile and printed in 1674 as 'Meditatien over eenige van 's Heeren Gods Eygenschappen'.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS  
**Project-level:** yes

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-CRIT-2005-INTRO, SRC-L-BUIJNSTERS-1971, SRC-MEDITATIEN-1674, SRC-L-1676-HERT-I-78-81

- The 1676 section heading for items 34-37 matches the reported 1674 pamphlet title almost verbatim, which supports identifying the two.
- The 1674 pamphlet itself is NOT_ACQUIRED; its exact contents and whether II was present in it are unverified.
- This is chronological corroboration for a 1674 date and an argument against 1664; it is not a reading of the print.

**Evidence gap:** No copy, catalogue record or page image of the 1674 'Meditatien' pamphlet has been acquired; its contents are known only through two modern descriptions.

**Next check:** Acquire the 1674 pamphlet (STCN / KB / UBA / Delpher) and collate its four poems against the 1676 printing, recording whether II is present and how it is dated.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-004-TEXT — DUTCH-004 source text and title

**Claim:** Den nedrigen christen, Op Psalm 131 is Lodenstein's seven-stanza expansion of Psalm 131, printed in Uyt-spanningen (Utrecht: Willem Clerck, 1676) on pp. 74-75, and reprinted with only orthographic variation in the 1727 ninth edition on the same pages.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-UYT-1727, SRC-L-CRIT-2005

- No editorial intervention for pp. 74-75 appears in the DBNL colophon, unlike the emended Rees dates.
- The 1727 edition varies in spelling and, in stanza IV, prints a question mark where 1676 prints an exclamation mark.
- Page images of pp. 74-75 were not inspected; the typography claim keeps LOCATOR_HOLD.

**HOLDs:** LOCATOR_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-004-DATE — DUTCH-004 dates

**Claim:** Den nedrigen christen was first published in 1676; its date of writing is unknown, and neither the 1676 nor the 1727 printing carries a date line.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-UYT-1727

- Do not infer a composition date from the publication year, and do not transfer the 1673-74 dating of the Rees group to this poem.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-004-PS131 — DUTCH-004 biblical anchor and genre

**Claim:** The poem is a poetic expansion, not a versification, of Psalm 131: it turns the psalm's assertions about a lowly heart and unlifted eyes into a request that God test the heart (stanzas I-II), and develops the weaned child of 131:2 into stanzas IV-V.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-CRIT-2005

- The 131:1-2 correspondences are demonstrable from the 1676 text itself.
- Mapping the closing stanza to Psalm 131:3 is this project's interpretation; it is consistent with, but not established by, the 2005 commentary, which is B1 and must not be the sole support.
- Classify as BIBLICAL_CANTICLE_OR_SCRIPTURE_EXPANSION, and note that it is nevertheless printed with a tune direction, i.e. meant for singing.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-004-TUNE — DUTCH-004 melody

**Claim:** The 1676 print directs the poem to the tune 'Prendete la' and resolves that designation by reference to printed p. 29 (Simeons Lof-sang); the tune's origin is untraced and no notation or independent identification is held.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-1676-SIMEONS-29, SRC-L-UYT-1727, SRC-L-CRIT-2005, SRC-NL-LIEDERENBANK

- The internal cross-reference works identically in the 1727 edition, where Simeons Lof-sang also stands on p. 29.
- The 2005 apparatus describes 'Prendete la' as an unknown tune designation pointing to the melody of text 9.
- The Dutch Song Database, the normal melody authority, was offline on 2026-10-25.
- A modern setting must never be presented as this tune.

**Evidence gap:** No melody source has been acquired: no notation, no identification of the tune's origin, and the Dutch Song Database is offline.

**Next check:** Query the Nederlandse Liederenbank when it returns online for 'Prendete la' and the DBNL song IDs lode002uyt_01_0025 and lode002uyt_03_0029; otherwise search 17th-century Dutch songbook collections for the tune name.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-004-RIGHTS — DUTCH-004 rights layers

**Claim:** The 1676 poem is a public-domain historical work, while the 2005 critical edition, the 1971 anthology and the DBNL transcription/images are distinct modern objects with their own rights; a new Russian adaptation may be made from the historical text but must not reproduce the modern editions.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-CRIT-2005, SRC-L-BUIJNSTERS-1971, SRC-L-1676-COLOPHON

- The 2005 edition prints 'Alle rechten voorbehouden' on its copyright page and is marked copyright-protected by DBNL.
- Rights in the underlying work do not extend to the modern edition's text, notes, or its editorial expansion of the print's 'etc.' abbreviations.
- The DBNL rights statement for the 1676 transcription has not been captured as an object, so the registry keeps RIGHTS_UNKNOWN with a RIGHTS_HOLD.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-NL-LIEDERENBANK-OFFLINE — Access state of the Dutch Song Database

**Claim:** On 2026-10-25 the Nederlandse Liederenbank returned HTTP 403 with a KNAW notice that the site has been taken offline temporarily, so DBNL's per-song links into it do not resolve.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS  
**Project-level:** yes

**Source IDs:** SRC-NL-LIEDERENBANK

- Recorded so that a later agent does not treat a dead-end link as evidence that the tune has no record.
- Re-test before concluding anything about a tune.

**HOLDs:** ARCHIVE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DBNL-SLUG-TRAP — DBNL locator convention

**Claim:** The numeric slug of a DBNL lode002uyt_01_01_00NN.php URL is a sequential chunk number, not a printed page number: slug 0036 shows printed pp. 74-75, slug 0039 shows pp. 82-84, and slug 0084 shows printed p. 230.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS  
**Project-level:** yes

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676, SRC-L-1676-COLOPHON, SRC-L-1676-DEN-NEDRIGEN, SRC-L-1676-HERT-II-82-84

- Reading the colophon's 'p. 84' as slug 0084 lands on an unrelated funeral poem. Cite the printed page bracket and the poem title.
- Keep observed printed-page markers separate from pagination inferred from illustration filenames. This warning is not a complete slug-to-page map and does not attest a fresh reading of DBNL or page images.

**HOLDs:** None recorded.

## Revised claims — 2026-10-25

### CLM-HERT-I-VS-II — Two poems titled Hert-sterckte in Jehova

Restated: Lodenstein's collection contains two distinct poems called Hert-sterckte in Jehova: I on printed pp. 78-81 (15 stanzas, critical-edition item 34, dated 28 December 1673) and II on printed pp. 82-84 (14 stanzas, item 35, dated 4 January 1674 in the later witnesses), which is the poem in the chain to Rutgers's 1806 hymn 68.

Review state: **SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS**. HOLDs: RIGHTS_HOLD.

- Both poems are in the 1676 first edition, not only in the critical edition and the 1971 selection.
- Keep the two poems separate. I has its own two tune names and must not be treated as the source text of the current Russian hymn; II has no tune of its own and points to I.
- Corrected on 2026-10-25: an earlier catalogue line cited the item-34 page as the source of II.

### CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1674 — Date of II. Hert-sterckte in Jehova

Restated: The date recorded for II. Hert-sterckte in Jehova is 4 January 1674. The 1676 transcription reads 1674 but is emended, the 1971 anthology prints 1674 while noting that the print reads 1664, and the 1727 edition independently prints 1674.

Review state: **EVIDENCE_HOLD**. HOLDs: EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD.

- Retained on EVIDENCE_HOLD but narrowed on 2026-10-25: what remains unresolved is only the literal reading of the 1676 impression, not the date's use.
- Scoped away from the p. 87 correction, which belongs to item 36 (see CLM-HERT-EMENDATION-SCOPE).
- The hold applies solely to the reading of the printed year. It does not suspend the poem's text, authorship, place, tune or its use as the DUTCH-002 source.
- The date is usable with an explicit note; see CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1727-WITNESS and CLM-REES-MEDITATIEN-1674.

**Evidence gap:** No page image of the 1676 leaf at printed p. 84 has been inspected. DBNL serves page images only for emblem pages and printed p. 84 carries none; its 'Scans' bundle for the edition was not acquired, and direct download from dbnl.org is blocked in this environment.

**Next check:** Inspect printed p. 84 in a physical or digitised copy of the 1676 impression: KB Den Haag 4 B 1 (the copy DBNL used for its images), UBA 113: BALIE 250.D12 (the copy transcribed), the DBNL scan bundle https://www.dbnl.org/arch/lode002uyt_01_01/pag/lode002uyt_01_01.pdf, or a Delpher/Google Books copy. Record the image and the reading before lifting the hold.

### CLM-DUTCH-002-RUTGERS — Source of Russian hymn Блаженно, блаженно — быть ничем

Restated: The Russian version follows the later seven-stanza Zelfverloochening in Evangelische Gezangen (1806), hymn LXVIII, associated with Jodocus van Lodenstein material revised by Abraham Rutgers; it is not a word-for-word translation of the whole 1674 poem.

Review state: **SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS**. HOLDs: RIGHTS_HOLD.

- Credit Lodenstein as historical source author and Rutgers as later reviser; treat later translations/adaptations as separate textual witnesses.
- The separate date uncertainty is isolated in CLM-HERT-II-DATE-1674; it does not automatically suspend the source-chain claim.
- Not re-examined in the 2026-10-25 third pass: the Rutgers link keeps its previous status.

## Fourth-pass additions — 2026-10-25 (supplementary brief)

## CLM-DUTCH-001-ADDRESSES — DUTCH-001: eight distinct Christological addresses

**Claim:** Schepselen Niet en Iesus Al varies its Christological address in every stanza (soet, lief, schoon, minlijckst, cierlijckst, soetste, waardigst) and pairs it with a different verb of attraction, so the address is not a refrain but part of each stanza's argument.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-SCHEPSELEN-131-134

**Notes:** Confirmed from the 1676 print, not from the critical edition. A translation that holds one address constant across all eight stanzas is a documented deviation, not a neutral choice. The address pair sits inside the stanza; the stanza's argument continues after it.

**Limits / next action:** See the dossier cited above.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-001-TRANSLATION-DECISIONS — DUTCH-001: reciprocal vow, favour/disfavour, sanctify-or-kill

**Claim:** The clause 'Iesu! wilt gy my' is the first half of a reciprocal vow completed by 'ick wil u, wil u alleen'; stanza II contrasts human favour with human disfavour as two ways the heart is stolen, not public opinion; and stanza IV asks Christ's love, cross and crown to sanctify the speaker's love or kill it.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-SCHEPSELEN-131-134

**Notes:** All three readings rest on the printed text and are recorded as working translation decisions in DOSSIER_DUTCH-001_TRANSLATION_DECISIONS.md. The reciprocal-vow reading is internal-evidence only and still needs a WNT check of the formula. No Russian text is in this repository, so no translation has been checked against these readings.

**Limits / next action:** See the dossier cited above.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-001-ARCHAIC-LEXIS — DUTCH-001: eight archaic expressions awaiting a lexical check

**Claim:** The expressions 'costelijck kuys', 'Reyn', "in 't dorre stickt", 'Leckre tong', 'schemer-ligt', 'Geven op de hand', 'Tegenwoordicheyd' and "d'onsigtbaarheden" all occur in the 1676 print at identifiable lines, but none has been resolved against a historical dictionary in this project.

**Review state:** RESEARCH_OPEN

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-SCHEPSELEN-131-134

**Notes:** Literal working glosses are recorded in the dossier with a confidence rating per item; 'in 't dorre stickt' and 'Geven op de hand' are the least settled. Not consulted in this pass: WNT. The 2005 apparatus would help but is copyrighted and may be paraphrased only. A glossary claim becomes usable only with a named headword and sense.

**Limits / next action:** Look up each expression in the WNT (headword and sense), record the result in the dossier, and only then promote each gloss from PENDING_LEXICAL_CHECK to a decision.

**Evidence gap:** No historical dictionary was consulted for any of the eight expressions.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-002-STANZAS-14-7-5 — DUTCH-002: stanza counts across the three forms

**Claim:** The 1676 poem has 14 stanzas, the 1806 hymn Zelfverloochening has seven, and the 1938 Gezang 205 has five; the 1938 form omits 1806 stanzas 4 and 5 but retains the last couplet of stanza 5 as the close of its own fourth stanza.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HERT-II-82-84, SRC-RUTGERS-1806-68, SRC-HB-1938-205-WEB, SRC-HB-1938-PRINT

**Notes:** This corrects an earlier project statement that the 1806 hymn has five stanzas. The 1676 and 1806 counts are verified from the prints. The 1938 count and its composition rest on two agreeing web transcriptions and are NOT yet verified against the printed bundel. The 1806 form is a reworking that drops the autobiographical exile frame of 1676 stanzas I, III and XIII, not a stanza-subset.

**Limits / next action:** Collate Gezang 205 against the printed 1938 bundel (Delpher identifier MMKB06:000000593) and record the page image number.

**Evidence gap:** The printed 1938 Hervormde Bundel has not been collated; only third-party web transcriptions were read.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-002-EXPRESSIONS — DUTCH-002: zielverdervend eigen, heylsaam Niet, Alles hoort den Hemel toe, Voor 't geloof is alles goed

**Claim:** 'Zielverdervend eigen' names soul-destroying self-will rather than mere possession; 'heylsaam Niet' is a 1676 phrase that does not survive into 1806; 'Alles hoort den Hemel toe' moves from the opening of 1676 stanza V to the close of 1806 stanza 3; and 'Voor 't geloof is alles goed' predicates goodness of the execution of God's counsel, not of the believer's circumstances.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-RUTGERS-1806-68, SRC-L-1676-HERT-II-82-84

Wat de Wijsheid zelv' beslist'), which is why the line cannot be reduced to a general consolation. The 1938 form replaces 'zielverdervend' with 'trots, hoogmoedig', weakening the diagnostic claim; a translation must record which form it follows. Detailed analysis and the full 1806 text of the stanza are in DOSSIER_DUTCH-002_TEXT_HISTORY.md.

## CLM-DUTCH-003-SONNET-DRAMATURGY — DUTCH-003: the sonnet's structure, turn and closing line

**Claim:** Hy droech onse smerten is a fourteen-line sonnet: lines 1-4 negate the Jewish participants, 5-8 the soldiers, line 9 is the turn to the first person ('Ick bent, o Heer, ick bent die u dit heb gedaen'), lines 10-13 make the speaker the tree, rope, nail, spear, scourge and blood-dropped crown in that order, and line 14 assigns the Passion's occurrence to the speaker's sins.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-R-HY-DROEG

**Notes:** Verified line by line against the 1930-1935 scholarly edition; the 1630 leaf is still not acquired. The apparatus records one first-edition reading indirectly: '8. A heeft een drukfout: tuyschen'. The closing line's force is causal ('om mijne sonden'); the discussed Russian turn 'я виновник мук Твоих' converts the original's agentive claim into a claim of causation and must be recorded as a deviation if adopted.

**Limits / next action:** Inspect the 1630 leaf, using the recorded 'tuyschen' misprint at line 8 to confirm the impression, and collate lines 1, 8, 9, 13 and 14.

**Evidence gap:** The 1630 first edition has not been inspected; only the 1930-1935 collation and its apparatus are held.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, LOCATOR_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-HEERLIJCKHEYD-IDENTITY — Identification of 'Heerlijckheyds Loff'

**Claim:** 'Heerlijckheyds Loff' ('Hoog! om hoog! mijn siel, na boven!') is a distinct ten-stanza poem by Lodenstein, printed in Uyt-spanningen (1676) on pp. 155-158, dated 27 August 1665, with the tune line 'Voyse: Un jour &c.' and critical-edition item 56; it is not a variant of any catalogued work and not part of another composition.

**Notes:** The earlier uncertainty is closed: every identifying feature in the working notes matches this text, and the Russian stanzas VII-X correspond to the Dutch stanzas VIII-X, which address Beauty and close doxologically. It shares vocabulary and the 'leven / lieven / loven' triad with DUTCH-001's poem but is a different work with a different title, date, tune line and stanza count. Registered as backlog candidate DUTCH-CAND-021, not as a production track.

**Limits / next action:** See the dossier cited above.

## CLM-HEERLIJCKHEYD-RECEPTION — Transmission of 'Heerlijckheyds Loff' and the 1938 trap

**Claim:** The poem was reprinted as 'Heemels heerlijkheids lof' in Stapel's Lusthof (1681) and as 'HEERLYKHEITS LOF' in the Bundeltje (1718), and the 1938 Hervormde Bundel contains a modernised reworking with a different incipit ('t Oog omhoog, het hart naar boven!) and a melody attributed to Loys Bourgeois.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_BY_SECONDARY_SOURCE_NEEDS_COLLATION  (`projectLevel: true` — concerns a text that is not a production work)

**Source IDs:** SRC-STAPEL-1681-LUSTHOF, SRC-NL-LIEDERENBANK-9086, SRC-NL-LIEDERENBANK-INDEX, SRC-PSALMENGEZ-1938-75, SRC-HB-1938-PRINT

**Notes:** The 1681 heading and incipit are located in the DBNL transcription (Claes Stapel, p. 495); the two-column layout prevents reliable full-text collation. The 1718 entry is still a catalogue lead. The 1938 reworking is a separate modern object with its own rights questions and must never be quoted as the 1665 poem. The Bourgeois melody attribution is a 20th-century hymnal attribution, not evidence of the poem's original tune.

**Limits / next action:** Inspect the 1681 page image (p. 495), the 1718 Bundeltje (p. 230, no. 56) and the actual page for Gezang 75 (catalogue: p. 501). Do not substitute the other hymn on images 724–725.

**Evidence gap:** The 1718 printing and the page for Gezang 75 have not been inspected. The existing Delpher reading of Gezang 205 does not verify Gezang 75 or its melody credit; the 1665 tune remains untraced.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## Fifth-pass additions and revisions — 2026-10-25

## CLM-HB-1938-205-CREDITS — Gezang 205 in the 1938 bundel: melody credit, text credit and rights

**Claim:** The printed 1938 Hervormde Bundel credits the melody of Gezang 205 to Johann Christoph Bach (1642-1703) and its text to Jodocus van Lodenstein with a reworking by Abraham Rutgers; the bundel prints the composer above the item and the author below it, a convention visible across neighbouring gezangen. The object is marked copyright-protected.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-HB-1938-PRINT-COLLATED, SRC-HB-1938-PRINT-CALIBRATION

**Notes:** Credits recorded in the earlier Delpher collation at image locators 724–725; the page-level OCR is incomplete for the sung text. No new image collation. The convention explains how a 16th- or 18th-century tune name comes to sit above a 20th-century hymnal item, and is the mechanism behind the reported Bourgeois attribution of the 1938 't Oog omhoog. The Bach attribution is a 1938 hymnal credit: it is not the 1674 tune, which the 1676 print names as 'Onherroepelijke Jeugd' / 'Gavotte. A la fronté' of poem I. Delpher states 'Auteursrechtelijk beschermd. Op dit object rust auteursrecht.'

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DELPHER-PAGE-OCR — Access route for printed Dutch hymnbooks on Delpher

**Claim:** Delpher serves page-level OCR text for a digitised book at a URL of the form '/nl/boeken/view?coll=boeken&identifier=<OBJECT>:<IMAGE>', where the image number is not the printed page number; items must be located by calibration. In-bundle search endpoints return 404, so a page must be found by bracketing.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS  (`projectLevel: true` — an access/method fact, not a text claim)

**Source IDs:** SRC-HB-1938-PRINT-COLLATED, SRC-HB-1938-PRINT-CALIBRATION

**Notes:** Calibration for the 1938 bundel: image 430 = Gezang 18, 640 = Gezang 153, 719 = 200, 721 = 202, 723 = 204, 724-725 = 205; image 296 = Psalm 109. '/nl/boeken/objectsearch/results' and '/nl/boeken/download/txt' both returned 404 in this pass. The OCR is lossy: it dropped a whole stanza on p. 724 and reorders some columns.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## Sixth-pass additions — 2026-10-25 (DUTCH-005 activated)

## CLM-DUTCH-005-TEXT — DUTCH-005: text, structure and date of 'Heerlijckheyds Loff'

**Claim:** Heerlijckheyds Loff is a ten-stanza poem printed in Uyt-spanningen (1676) on pp. 155-158, dated 27 August 1665. Each stanza is printed as four statement lines, a call line and its abbreviated repeat, a tripled word with its object, the call again, and a closing line; the tripled word runs 'leven' (III-IV-V-VI) -> 'lieven' (VII-VIII-IX) -> 'loven' (X).

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HEERLIJCKHEYD-155-158, SRC-L-CRIT-2005-HEERLIJCKHEYD

**Notes:** Read in full from the diplomatic transcription in the fifth pass; the whole text has now been seen, but not collated against a page image. The print abbreviates repeated call lines as 'etc.'; these are repeat instructions, not lacunae. Stanza I is printed in full and is the metrical model for the rest. The page carries an illustration (ill0155) that has not been viewed; no claim is made about it. Line I.7 ('Is uw costlijck leven, lieven, loven') has no expressed negation; the elliptical reading is adopted in the production package, with the 'costly' pun recorded as a secondary sense.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-005-TUNE — DUTCH-005: the printed tune direction is untraceable

**Claim:** The printed direction reads 'Voyse: Un jour &c.', but no notation is held, the Dutch Song Database was unreachable, and the 2005 apparatus states that the melody's origin is unknown and that it is not the 'Un jour &c.' of text 27. No melody claim can therefore be made for this poem.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HEERLIJCKHEYD-155-158, SRC-L-CRIT-2005-HEERLIJCKHEYD, SRC-NL-LIEDERENBANK-INDEX

**Notes:** The tune name is a designation the modern editors could not resolve, so it must not be presented as an identified melody. The 1938 hymnal's melody attribution for its reworking of this text (Loys Bourgeois) is a different, 20th-century object and is not evidence for the 1665 tune.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-005-ADAPTATION — DUTCH-005: the Russian text is a declared adaptation, not a translation

**Claim:** The Russian lyric for DUTCH-005 is an independent literary adaptation that follows the ten stanzas in order and preserves the tripled word, with eight declared deviations (supplied negation in I.7, noun instead of infinitive in the tripled device, 'costelijck' as 'precious', expanded 'etc.' repeats, no invented refrains, 'Cijter' as 'цитра', an inline repeat in VI.5, and no metre or rhyme claim). No part of it may be presented as a translation, and it is not approved.

**Review state:** REPORTED_NOT_INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HEERLIJCKHEYD-155-158

**Notes:** Draft v0.1 authored in this repository on 2026-10-25; the owner's poetic revision is a required gate before READY_FOR_REVIEW. The deviation table is in SUNO/DUTCH-005_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE.md §5. Nothing has been submitted to SUNO: the style prompt is AUTHORED_NOT_YET_SUBMITTED and no generation exists.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-005-VARIANTS — DUTCH-005: variants between the 1676 and 1727 printings

**Claim (revised 2026-10-26, full-text collation):** The two printings differ in five substantive places: 1676 I.3 reads 'leven, lieven, loven' where 1727 reads 'leven, leven, loven'; 1676 VII.8 reads 'Mijn siel na boven dat' where 1727 reads 'dan', matching line 5; 1727 adds 'al' in VIII.2 ('van al wat hier schoon schijnt'); and 1727 omits the abbreviated repeat line in stanza VIII, printing eight lines instead of nine. The rest is spelling only.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-1676-HEERLIJCKHEYD-155-158, SRC-L-UYT-1727-HEERLIJCKHEYD

**Notes:** Read directly from both transcriptions, in full; no page image was inspected for either. The fifth variant — 1676 VII.3 `Vindt` against 1727 `Vind` — was found in the 2026-10-26 collation, where the 2005 edition agrees with 1676. See `COLLATION_DUTCH-005_THREE_WAY.md`. The dating form differs cosmetically: '27. in Ougstmaand 1665' (1676) and '27 in Oogstmaand 1665' (1727). The tripled device is unaffected: both print leven/leven/leven, lieven/lieven/lieven and loven/loven/loven in stanzas III-X. Because two impressions disagree, neither can be treated as the copy text without a page-image check.

**Evidence gap:** No page image of either printing has been inspected.

**Next check:** Inspect the 1676 page images of pp. 155-158 (and the 1727 printing where they differ) and record the readings of I.3, VII.3, VII.8, VIII.2 and the stanza-VIII line count. Until then no wording is called definitive.

## CLM-DUTCH-005-TUNE-SHIFT — DUTCH-005: the tune direction changes between 1676 and 1718

**Claim:** 1676 prints `Voyse: Un jour etc.`, 1681 `Un jour, &c.`; by 1718 the direction is Psalm 42, the 1806 hymn names its melody `Psalm 042 Datheen`, and the 1938 hymnal credits Louis Bourgeois — the composer to whom the Genevan psalm 42 melody is attributed. "Psalm 42" and "Bourgeois" therefore name one tune, not two rivals.

## CLM-DUTCH-005-EDITIONS — DUTCH-005: how often the collection was printed before 1800

**Claim:** Buijnsters's bibliography lists **nineteen printing records** of *Uyt-spanningen* before 1800, the last being the **sixteenth edition, 1780**. The second edition (Clerck, 1679) appeared **without music examples**, because the publisher had asked the musician **J.C. Valbeek** to write new melodies for most of the songs.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-BUIJNSTERS-1971

**Limits / next action:** Locator: bibliography in Buijnsters (ed.), *Bloemlezing uit de bundel Uyt-spanningen* (1971), pp. 24–25 (DBNL). The nineteen are printing records, not nineteen numbered editions: the numbered series reaches the sixteenth, because the 4th and 5th editions each survive in two printings and one 10th edition is undated. Copy must therefore say "nineteen printings, the sixteenth in 1780", never "nineteenth edition".

**Next check:** if a run of this hymn ever needs edition-level precision, distinguish the two 1683 and the two 1693–95 printings.

## CLM-L-UTRECHT-1653 — Lodenstein's Utrecht ministry: the year, and the 1644/1650/1653 discrepancy

**Claim:** The call to Utrecht reached Lodenstein as early as **1652**; after a period of illness he entered the Utrecht ministry in **April 1653** and served there until his death in **1677**. The years 1644, 1650 and 1653 that secondary sources give are not rival datings of one event but his three pastorates: Zoetermeer 1644, Sluis 1650, Utrecht 1653.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-CRIT-2005-INTRO

**Limits / next action:** Locator: A. Ros, «Ds. Jodocus van Lodenstein en zijn *Uyt-spanningen* 1676», in the 2005 critical edition, printed p. 645; the DBNL page was opened and read on 2026-10-25. It is a modern editor's biography (B1), not a contemporary witness, and it is copyrighted — paraphrase only, never quotation. It dates the ministry, not any individual poem. Hymnary's 1644/1650/1653 is not contradicted by this page, it is explained by it.

**Consequence for copy:** «с апреля 1653 года» is now allowed in the description and in the Telegram post. Before this page was opened, every form of «since 1653» was banned from the copy, and the ban was right.

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

## CLM-DUTCH-005-MELODY-REGISTER — the register files 27 and 56 together, and why that is not a contradiction

**Claim:** The 2005 melody register lists the direction `Un jour m'en alloit &c.` for texts **27 and 56** (56 is DUTCH-005), while the apparatus note on item 56 states that the melody there is of unknown origin and **is not** the `Un jour &c.` of text 27. Both hold: the register indexes printed direction strings, the note speaks about melodies.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-CRIT-2005-MELODIEREGISTER, SRC-L-CRIT-2005-HEERLIJCKHEYD

**Limits / next action:** Locators: register, printed pp. 663–664; the apparatus note verbatim on item 56, printed p. 263. Copy consequence: the register may be cited for "two poems, one direction", never for "two poems, one tune". The shipped sentence stands unchanged. This is the second time in this project that one melody under two names nearly became two rival melodies; the first was "Bourgeois or psalm 42".

**HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD

**Claim:** The register keys texts **9 and 32** twice: under `Prendete la` and under `Gy laat, dien gy vind rede staan`, which is item 9's own incipit. The second string is a second key to the same tune, not a rival tune; the tune's origin remains untraced.

## CLM-CAND-006-CRUYCE — 't Cruyce van een christen, item 57

**Claim:** Item 57 has **fifteen** four-line stanzas and a closing date line of **16 January 1659**, and stands in the 1676 print on **pp. 158–160**, directly under DUTCH-005's date line. Its tune direction, `'t En is niet alle tijd, &c.` with a reference to printed p. 100, is the tune of item 41 (*Jesus lijdende liefde*); the 2005 headnote identifies that incipit as the opening line of **Camphuysen's *Christelijcke Gelatenheydt***.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS

**Source IDs:** SRC-L-CRIT-2005, SRC-L-1676, SRC-L-CRIT-2005-MELODIEREGISTER

**Limits / next action:** Locators: 1676 text `lode002uyt_01_01_0062.php` (pp. 158–160); 2005 item 57, pp. 268–271; register pp. 663–664. The print abbreviates the closing couplet to `Dat is om uwent wil, O! etc.` in stanzas VI–XI and varies it otherwise, so an adaptation must neither add nor restore those lines. **Held:** the text layer stops before the final rhyme word of stanza XV (`mijn eer`), so the closing line needs the page image of p. 160. Camphuysen's song is named but not acquired, and no melody claim follows from the direction.

**HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD, RIGHTS_HOLD

## Ancient-hymn lane (PHOS-001) — added 2026-10-26

### CLM-PHOS-IDENTITY — text of the hymn

**Claim:** the received Greek text of Φῶς ἱλαρόν is six lines: address to Christ as the gladsome light of the holy glory of the immortal Father; the coming of sunset and the evening light; praise of Father, Son and Holy Spirit, God; the Son, giver of life, worthy to be hymned at all times, so the world glorifies him.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_BY_SECONDARY_SOURCE_NEEDS_COLLATION · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-CPDL, SRC-PHOS-PRAVENC · **HOLDs:** LOCATOR_HOLD
**Limits / next action:** read in a community transcription; collate against a printed Horologion or Plank 2001 before quoting it as "the received text".

### CLM-PHOS-BASIL-73 — Earliest testimony

**Claim:** In Blomfield Jackson's NPNF2 8 English translation (1895), as revised on New Advent, De Spiritu Sancto 29.73 says that the fathers gave thanks at evening light, that the author of the lamp-lighting thanksgiving cannot be named, and that the people utter an ancient formula: 'We praise Father, Son, and God's Holy Spirit'. The Hymn of Athenogenes is named in the next sentence, not identified with that thanksgiving.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DSS, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-PG32-OCR, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DATE · **HOLDs:** RIGHTS_HOLD
**Notes:** The claim is explicitly about the passage as read in the English web edition, not a verbatim transcription of the Greek or a quotation of all six hymn lines; The Greek passage has now been read in PG 32 OCR only, including readable Θεοῦ; no scan, critical apparatus or manuscript collation. The approximate date belongs to the separately identified dating source; No EVIDENCE_HOLD is mechanically added just because the whole treatise is unread: the evidence claim is limited to the read passage and language. Publication and rights decisions remain separate.

### CLM-PHOS-AUTHORSHIP — Authorship

**Claim:** The original author is not established. In the NPNF rendering Basil cannot name the author of the lamp-lighting thanksgiving and names the Hymn of Athenogenes separately; the claim that this proves Athenogenes wrote Phos Hilaron is not warranted. Attribution to Sophronius cannot explain the pre-seventh-century thanksgiving attested by Basil; this does not rule out later redaction of the received text. The 500–699 catalogue date of Papyrus London 2037 b alone does not refute seventh-century authorship.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-PRAVENC, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DSS, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-PG32-OCR, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-DCLP, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-MILNE1927 · **HOLDs:** ARCHIVE_HOLD
**Notes:** The encyclopaedia reports that the Athenogenes ascription resulted from conflating two testimonies. The juxtaposition is read in Basil; the historical origin of the attribution has not independently been traced through manuscripts; Papyrus identity and catalogue date are checked in DCLP/LDAB, and the extent is now described in Milne 1927 no. 244, read in OCR. The papyrus letters, images and palaeography are not examined. A seventh-century fragment can carry earlier or contemporary words; this does not identify their author; Public copy and any attribution/redaction decision remain outside this research update.
**Limits / next action:** Basil quotes a Trinitarian clause, not the six lines; exclude authorship of the ancient thanksgiving, not every possible later revision; ARCHIVE_HOLD remains for the unexamined papyrus and attribution-bearing manuscript/edition evidence.

### CLM-PHOS-AC-CHECKED — Подтверждает ли ссылка на Апостольские постановления VIII.35 запись Phos Hilaron?

**Claim:** Нет: в прочитанной английской веб-передаче Donaldson / New Advent VIII.34–37 даёт часы молитвы, вечернее собрание, неназванный псалом при возжении светильников, прошения и благодарение, но не текст Phos Hilaron. В дополнительно прочитанных VII.47–49 находятся утреннее славословие, вечерняя молитва с псалмовой хвалой и песнью Симеона, затем молитва перед трапезой — также не этот гимн. Отрицательный результат относится к указанным местам, не к полному просмотру всех книг или греческой рукописной традиции.

**Review state:** VERIFIED_NEGATIVE · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-AC-ANF, SRC-PHOS-AC-BOOK7, SRC-PHOS-MESSENGER-ACHYMNS, SRC-PHOS-PRAVENC · **HOLDs:** none
**Notes:** 18.11.2026 перечитаны VII.47–49 и VIII.34–37 с границей VIII.38, по закреплённому HTML-зеркалу; выходные данные дополнительно прочитаны на живых страницах. Переводчик James Donaldson, ANF 7 (1886), не Schaff и не NPNF. Полные книги не прочитаны; Положительный контроль — VII.47 действительно содержит утреннее славословие (CLM-PHOS-GLORIA-AC); вечерний псалом в VIII.35 не назван и не может быть отождествлён с Phos Hilaron по одному предписанию петь; Прежнее объяснение «отсюда и пошла ошибка» заменено: сходство обряда может объяснять путаницу, но история её возникновения и распространения здесь не прослежена. Поисковая передача Messenger (C / LINK_ONLY) не считается независимо прочитанным подтверждением; VERIFIED_NEGATIVE сохранён только для проверки названных локаторов. RIGHTS_HOLD у двух источников ограничивает перепечатку веб-редакции; собственный пересказ результата не объявляет эту редакцию лицензированной.
**Limits:** Английская веб-редакция, не критический греческий текст и не скан ANF. Универсальное отсутствие гимна во всём сочинении этим проходом не доказывается; Греческие названия псалма и песней в досье — идентификационные обозначения, а не результат греческой сверки. Ни время возникновения гимна, ни генеалогия популярной ошибки из этих отрывков не следуют; Отсутствие цитаты в уставном предписании не доказывает, что гимн не мог исполняться при обряде; здесь проверяется наличие текста по указанной ссылке, не история исполнения.
**Next check:** Если потребуется утверждение о греческом тексте, сверить VII.47–49 и VIII.34–38 по точно идентифицированному критическому изданию; для более широкого отрицания сначала определить и действительно прочитать полный требуемый объём.

### CLM-PHOS-OLDEST-SCOPE — The scope of the word 'oldest'

**Claim:** The qualified description of Phos Hilaron as the earliest non-scriptural hymn preserved complete and sung continuously is a comparative literature claim still requiring verification, not a conclusion established by Basil 29.73. Basil supplies an ancient lamp-lighting formula; he does not establish six-line textual identity or continuous performance. The selected hymn is not claimed to be the oldest by manuscript or composition.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_BY_SECONDARY_SOURCE_NEEDS_COLLATION · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DSS, SRC-PHOS-PRAVENC, SRC-PHOS-POXY, SRC-PHOS-ODES, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-MILNE1927 · **HOLDs:** EVIDENCE_HOLD
**Limits / next action:** The earlier formulation and its permission to use the qualified superlative are superseded here, not silently treated as verified. The comparison with older hymns in the source list was not an exhaustive demonstration; EVIDENCE_HOLD applies to the comparative superlative and continuity, not to the existence of the read Basil passage. The papyrus facsimile was not acquired; the limited OCR publication is recorded separately below. Track selection is unchanged; no product copy has been edited; Milne 1927 no. 244 is now read in OCR: it is not a complete six-line witness (CLM-PHOS-PAPYRUS-EXTENT). The material basis for the previous completeness argument has therefore been narrowed, not verified. EVIDENCE_HOLD remains.

### CLM-PHOS-NOT-ALEXANDRINUS — the Codex Alexandrinus claim, dropped

**Claim:** that the hymn's oldest text is in the 5th-century Codex Alexandrinus is **false**: its fourteen odes after the Psalter end with the Ὕμνος ἑωθινός (Gloria), and the hymn is not among them (British Library, Royal MS 1 D VII).

**Review state:** `VERIFIED_NEGATIVE` — looked for in a primary text and found not to be the case · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-BL-ALEX, SRC-PHOS-ODES-BOOK
**Limits:** from the library's description and the standard list of odes; the codex was not examined page by page. The confusion is with the Gloria, which does have a 5th-century Alexandrian witness.

### CLM-PHOS-BASIL-SCOPE — What Basil does and does not attest

**Claim:** In the NPNF English translation, Basil attests the antiquity of a lamp-lighting thanksgiving and quotes a Trinitarian clause, not the complete received six-line Phos Hilaron. Neither its full wording in c. 375 nor the date of composition or a complete textual transmission can be inferred from this passage.

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DSS, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-PG32-OCR, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DATE, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-MILNE1927 · **HOLDs:** none
**Limits / next action:** The Greek comparison is confined to OCR of the printed passage, not a verified Greek transcription. Several OCR versions of PG 32 are not independent textual witnesses; The former statement that the complete six-line text is first read in a 6th–7th-century papyrus exceeded access and is superseded. DCLP has an empty edition block; the subsequent OCR reading of Milne no. 244 instead describes a strip and prints two incomplete lines (CLM-PHOS-PAPYRUS-EXTENT). No complete early text or facsimile reading is claimed.

### CLM-PHOS-DATING-OPEN — Date of composition

**Claim:** The date of composition is unknown. In the read NPNF passage Basil treats the lamp-lighting thanksgiving/formula as ancient c. 375. The papyrus witness reported in the literature is now identified in DCLP as London 2037 b = P. Lond. Lit. 244, dated 500–699 by the catalogue, not palaeographically verified here.

**Review state:** RESEARCH_OPEN · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-BASIL-DSS, SRC-PHOS-PRAVENC, SRC-PHOS-BASIL-PG32-OCR, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-DCLP, SRC-PHOS-PAPLOND2037B-MILNE1927 · **HOLDs:** ARCHIVE_HOLD
**Notes:** The popular '200 AD or earlier' figure was not verified in this pass and is not used; Do not present any second- or third-century date as established; A catalogue date of a copy is neither a date of composition nor a firsthand reading of six complete lines. Milne no. 244 is now read in OCR, reporting a sixth/seventh-century strip and printing two incomplete lines, not a complete text. The papyrus and page images remain unread.

### CLM-PHOS-POXY-TEXT — the state of the Oxyrhynchus hymn

**Claim:** P.Oxy. XV 1786 (c. 250–300) preserves lyrics and Greek vocal notation, but the text is fragmentary: five lines with lacunae; the sense is a call for cosmic silence while Father, Son and Holy Spirit are hymned, ending «Amen, amen».

**Review state:** SUPPORTED_WITH_SCOPE_LIMITS · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-POXY · **Level:** series (roadmap)
**Limits:** the editions (Grenfell–Hunt 1922, Cosgrove 2011) were not opened; a modern reconstruction is the editor's text and is not public domain.

### CLM-PHOS-SUBTUUM-DATING — the date of *Sub tuum praesidium*

**Claim:** contested, and must be stated with both sides: Rylands 470 dated 3rd c. (Lobel) or 4th (Roberts); Förster argues 8th and denies a consensus; Towarek puts the earliest textual witness in the 6th–7th c.; the Georgian Iadgari shows liturgical use in 5th-century Jerusalem.

**Review state:** REPORTED_NOT_INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED · **Sources:** SRC-PHOS-SUBTUUM · **Level:** series (roadmap)
**Notes:** Состояние спора уточнено чтением 2026-10-27: см. CLM-PHOS-SUBTUUM-DATING-STATE — Stegmüller (нач. IV в. с допуском до VI), Förster (VI–VII или VIII–IX; de Bruyn, Effenberger согласны), Михайко 2019 (VIII–IX), Trismegistos (700–900); Towarek 2021: по Фёрстеру, древнейший свидетель ныне — венская запись VI–VII вв. (MPER N.S. XVII, № 29). Ранее утверждение «between the 3rd and 7th centuries depending on the scholar» скорректировано: нижняя граница III в. не держится.;
**Limits:** secondary source only. Copy may say «between the 3rd and 7th centuries depending on the scholar», never a single date.
